CORRIGENDA
IN Gazette Notice No. 14238 of 2024, amend the expression
printed as “NDIRANGU GACHUNIA, CECM, Physical Planning and
Urban Development, Migori County” to read “NDIRANGU
GACHUNIA, CECM, Physical Planning and Urban Development,
Nyeri County”.
------------------
IN Gazette Notice No. 13639 of 2024, amend the expression
printed as “Cause No. E446 of 2024” to read “Cause No. E466 of
2024”.
------------------
IN Gazette Notice No. 1620 of 2022, amend the expression printed
as “Cause No. E43 of 2022” to read “Cause No. E45 of 2022” and
“Cause No. E45 of 2022” to read “Cause No. E43 of 2022”.
------------------
IN Gazette Notice No. 2730 of 2015, amend the expression printed
as “L.R. No. 12862 by virtue of a certificate of title registered as I.R.
36738,” to read “L.R.A. Nos. 12408 and 12862 by virtue of a
certificate of title registered as I.R. Nos. 32126 and 36738”.
------------------
IN Gazette Notice No. 8843 of 2021, Cause No. E17 of 2021,
amend the expression printed as “grant of letters of administration
intestate,” to read “grant of probate of written will”.
------------------
IN Gazette Notice No. 5049 of 2024, amend the proprietor’s name
printed as “Teresia Wanja Simon Ndung’u,” to read “Teresia Wanja
Simon Ndugu”.
------------------
In Gazette Notice No. 12635 of 2024, delete “Rosemary Kemunto
Onkoba”.
------------------
IN Gazette Notice No. 8051 of 2024, Cause No. E285 of 2023,
amend the expression printed as “IN THE HIGH COURT OF KENYA
AT NAIROBI” to read “IN THE CHIEF MAGISTRATE’S COURT
AT NAIROBI”.
GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15333
ECONOMIC PARTNERSHIP AGREEMENT (EPA)
BETWEEN
THE REPUBLIC OF KENYA, A MEMBER OF THE EAST
AFRICAN COMMUNITY, OF THE ONE PART, AND THE
EUROPEAN UNION, OF THE OTHER PART
THE EPA COUNCIL
APPOINTMENT
PURSUANT to the establishment of the EPA Council in the
Economic Partnership Agreement between Kenya, a Member of the
East African Community, on the one part, and the European Union, on
the other part; it is notified for the general information of the public
that five appointed members of the EPA Council representing Kenya
shall be the following:
EPA Council
Salim Mvurya – Cabinet Secretary Investment,
Trade and Industry – Co-
Chairperson,
Members:
Musalia Mudavadi (Dr.) – Prime Cabinet Secretary and
Cabinet Secretary Foreign and
Diaspora Affairs,
John Mbadi – Cabinet Secretary, The National
Treasury and Economic
Planning,
Andrew Mwihia Karanja (Dr.) – Cabinet Secretary, Agriculture
and Livestock Development,
Aden Duale – Cabinet Secretary, Environment,
Climate Change and Forestry.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.
WILLIAM SAMOEI RUTO,
President.
GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15334
THE STATE CORPORATIONS ACT
(Cap. 446)
THE MWAI KIBAKI REFERRAL HOSPITAL ORDER, 2024
MWAI KIBAKI REFERRAL HOSPITAL
APPOINTMENT
IN EXERCISE of the powers conferred by section 6 (1) (a) of the
Mwai Kibaki Referral Hospital Order, 2024, I, William Samoei Ruto,
President of the Republic of Kenya and Commander-in-Chief of the
Defence Forces, appoint—
GENERAL (RTD.) ROBERT KIBOCHI
to be the Chairperson of the Board of Directors of Mwai Kibaki
Referral Hospital, for a period of three (3) years, with effect from the
22nd November, 2024.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.
WILLIAM SAMOEI RUTO,
President.
GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15335
THE SUGAR ACT, 2024
KENYA SUGAR BOARD
APPOINTMENT
IN EXERCISE of the powers conferred by section 6 (1) (a) of the
Sugar Act, 2024, I, William Samoei Ruto, President of the Republic of
Kenya and Commander-in-Chief of the Defence Forces, appoint—
NICHOLAS ODERO GUMBO (ENG.)
to be the Chairperson of the Board of Directors, Kenya Sugar Board,
for a period of three (3) years, with effect from the 22nd November,
2024.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.
WILLIAM SAMOEI RUTO,
President.
GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15336
THE STATE CORPORATIONS ACT
(Cap. 446)
KENYA BIOVAX INSTITUTE LIMITED
APPOINTMENT
IN EXERCISE of the powers conferred by section 6 (1) (a) of the
State Corporations Act, I, William Samoei Ruto, President of the
Republic of Kenya and Commander-in-Chief of the Defence Forces,
appoint—
SWARUP RANJAN MISHRA (DR.)

22nd November, 2024.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.
WILLIAM SAMOEI RUTO,

GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15337
THE CIVIL PROCEDURE ACT
(Cap. 21)
APPOINTMENT
IT IS notified for the general knowledge of the public that—
WILLIS ODUOR OLUGA – ADVOCATE
has been appointed as a member of the Rules Committee of the
Judiciary under section 81 (1) (vi) of the Civil Procedure Act.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.
MARTHA K. KOOME,
Chief Justice and President of the Supreme Court of Kenya.
GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15338
THE AUCTIONEERS ACT
(Cap. 526)
APPOINTMENT
IN EXERCISE of the powers conferred by section 3 (1) (g) of the
Auctioneers Act, Cap. 526, laws of Kenya, the Chief Justice
appoints—
Edwin Okemwa Saisi,
Gladys Warigia Mwangi (Ms.),
to the Auctioneers Licensing Board, for a term of three (3) years, with
effect from 8th November, 2024. The appointment of Ericsson Obura
vide Gazette Notice No. 10126 of 2023, is revoked.
Dated the15th November, 2024.
MARTHA K. KOOME,
Chief Justice and President of the Supreme Court of Kenya.
GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15339
THE NATIONAL MUSEUMS AND HERITAGE ACT
(Cap. 216)
NATIONAL MUSEUMS OF KENYA
APPOINTMENT
IN EXERCISE of the powers conferred by section 6 (1) (b) of the
National Museums and Heritage Act, the Cabinet Secretary for
Gender, Culture, the Arts and Heritage appoints—
REUBEN KOIMA (DR.)
to be a member of the Board of Directors, the National Museums of
Kenya, for a period of three (3) years, with effect from the 22nd
November, 2024.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.
MUSALIA MUDAVADI,
Prime Cabinet Secretary and
Ag. Cabinet Secretary for Gender, Culture, the Arts and Heritage.

GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15351
MINISTRY OF YOUTH AFFAIRS, CREATIVE ECONOMY
AND SPORTS
GENDER PROTECTION AND IMPLEMENTATION COMMITEE
APPOINTMENT OF THE ORGANIZING COMMITTEE AND SECRETARIAT
FOR THE GENDER PROTECTION AND IMPLEMENTATION COMMITTEE
IT IS notified for general information that the Cabinet Secretary
for Youth Affairs, Creative Economy and Sports has appointed an
Advisory Board and Secretariat whose function will be to among
others ensure full implementation of the report on Gender Welfare and
Equity in Sports submitted by the taskforce on Gender Welfare in
Sports on 19th January, 2022, to facilitate development and
implementation as well as review of gender and Gender Based
Violence (GBV) policies, to participate in and/or facilitate institutional
gender analysis and gender audits, to advocate for gender
mainstreaming in sports and sports organizations and its membership
shall include the following—
(a) Advisory Board
(i) Cabinet Secretary for Youth Affairs, Creative Economy
and Sports
(ii) Principal Secretary, State Deparment for Sports
Members
(i) Paul Ochieng (Prof.)
(ii) Catherine Ndereba (Ms.)
(iii) Barbra Malowa(Ms.)
(iv) Essie Akida (Ms.)
(v) Benjamin Wafula
(vi) Elizabeth Wangeci Gichuki
(vii) Joyce Kebenei
(b) Secretariat
(i) Diana Watila-Chief Executive Officer (Ms.)
(ii) Catherine Njeri Maina (Ms.)
(iii) Lorna Tanui (Ms.)
(iv) Fardhosa Hassan Boru (Ms.)
(v) Patricia Arisi (Ms.)
(c) The Functions of the Steering Committee are:
(i) to ensure full implementation of the report on gender
welfare and equity in sports submitted by the taskforce on
gender welfare in sports on 19th January, 2022;
(ii) to facilitate development and implementation as well as
review of gender and Gender Based Violence policies;
(iii) to develop a framework for a multi-agency approach in
dealing with gender-based violence in sports and sports
organizations;
(iv) to advocate for gender mainstreaming in sports and sports
organizations;
(v) to participate in and/or facilitate institutional gender
analysis and gender audits;
(vi) to provide a safe environment for sports personnel in their
participation in sports activities;
(vii) to assesses the progress of gender-related initiatives and
evaluate their effectiveness over time;
(viii) to organize workshops, training sessions, or awareness
campaigns to educate members of the organization about
gender-related issues and unconscious bias;
(ix) to ensure existence of an elaborate and institutionalized
gender mainstreaming implementation structure;
(x) to coordinate reporting on the status of gender equality to
relevant structures on behalf of the institution;
(xi) to regularly review progress of planned gender
mainstreaming interventions within the sports sector;
22nd November, 2024 THE KENYA GAZETTE 5459
(xii) to receive and disseminate feedback on gender
mainstreaming on behalf of the institution and action as
appropriate;
(xiii) to undertake such other function, powers and activities as
the Cabinet Secretary may direct as may be incidental to;
The term of the Committee shall be a period of three (3) years
from the date hereof, and such term may be extended for such period
as the Cabinet Secretary may consider necessary.
The Committee in discharge of its functions shall except as
provided shall regulate its own procedure.
Dated the 19th November, 2024.
KIPCHUMBA MURKOMEN,
Cabinet Secretary for Youth Affairs,
Creative Economy and Sports.

GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15362
THE STATUTORY INSTRUMENTS ACT
(Cap. 2A)
REGULATORY IMPACT STATEMENT AND CALL FOR PUBLIC
PARTICIPATION
PURSUANT to the provisions of section 8 of the Statutory
Instruments Act, the Cabinet Secretary for Agriculture and Livestock
Development notifies tea industry stakeholders and the general public
that the Ministry has prepared the Regulatory Impact Statements for
the Tea (Levy) Regulations, 2024, the Tea (Licensing and
Registration) Regulations, 2024, and Corporate Governance
Guidelines and Code of Conduct for the Tea Sector.
In compliance with the Constitution and the Statutory Instruments
Act, the Ministry invites tea industry stakeholders, members of the
public, and all interested parties, to submit their comments, views and
inputs on the aforementioned Regulations, the Regulatory Impact
Statements and the Corporate Governance Guidelines and Code of
Conduct.
The documents have been prepared pursuant to the Tea Act as
follows:-
1. Tea (Levy) Regulations, 2024 and the Regulatory Impact
Statement on Regulations, 2024;
These regulations provide for the manner of levying and collecting
the tea levy in line with the powers given to the Cabinet Secretary
under Section 53 of the Tea Act No. 23 of 2020. The Regulatory
Impact Statement contains detailed information on the anticipated
impacts of the draft Tea (Tea Levy) Regulations, 2024.
2. The Tea (Registration and Licensing) Regulations, 2024 and
the Regulatory Impact Statement on the Regulations
The Purpose of these Regulations is to provide for the registration
and licensing of tea value chain players, the terms and conditions of
Licensing and Registration and appeal process in case of denial or
refusal. The Regulatory Impact Statement contains detailed
information on the anticipated impacts of the draft Tea (Registration
and Licensing) Regulations, 2024.
3. Corporate Governance Guidelines and Code of Conduct for the
Tea Sector and the Regulatory Impact Statement on the Guidelines and
Code of Conduct for the Tea Sector.
The purpose of the Corporate Governance Guidelines and Conduct
of Conduct is to provide for enhanced accountability in the tea sector
and align it with best corporate government practices to pave way for a
revitalized and ethically governed industry. The Regulatory Impact
Statement contains detailed information on the anticipated impacts of
the Corporate Governance Guidelines and Conduct of Conduct.
The above documents can be accessed on the Ministry website:
at www.kilimo.go.ke and at the Tea Board of Kenya website at
www.teaboard.or.ke.
The Ministry will also hold public forums to discuss the
documents. The proposed consultations are scheduled to be held as
follows:–

DELEGATION OF FUNCTIONS
PURSUANT to section 20 of the Urban Areas and Cities Act, 2011
and clause 5 of the Kinango Municipal Charter, the County Executive
Committee approves and delegates the following functions to be
undertaken by the Kinango Municipality, with effect from the 22nd

solid waste management services;
(b) Construction and maintenance of urban roads and associated
infrastructure;

controls;
(d) Construction and maintenance of walkways and other non-
motorized transport infrastructure;

spaces;
(f) Construction and maintenance of street lighting;
(g) Construction maintenance and regulation of traffic controls and
parking facilities;
(h) Construction and maintenance of bus stations and taxi stands;
(i) Promotion, regulation and provision of animal control and
welfare;
(j) Any other function as may be delegated by the County
Executive Committee.

(Cap. 41)

PURSUANT to sections 2 (3) of the Vexatious Proceedings Act in

Court (Organization and Administration) Act, Cap. 8 C, the Registrar,
High Court gives notice–
THAT the High Court has issued Vexatious Orders in Misc. Civil
Application No. E163 of 2021 as specified in the Schedule hereto:
SCHEDULE
REPUBLIC OF KENYA
IN THE HIGH COURT OF KENYA AT KISUMU
MISC. APPLICATION NO E163 OF 2021
HON. ATTORNEY GENERAL ………………APPLICANT
-VERSUS-
DAVID OSCRA OWAKO …………………....RESPONDENT
ORDER
(In open Court Before Hon Justice R.E Aburili on 24th May 2023)
THIS MATTER coming up for Judgement before Hon. Lady
Justice R.E. Aburili in the presence of Ms. Juma Litigation Counsel for
the Applicant, Mr. Otieno David Advocate for interested party and in
the absence of the Respondent.
IT IS HEREBY ORDERED
1. THAT the application by the Attorney General Pursuant to the
provisions of Section 2(1) and (3) of the Vexatious
Proceedings Act is hereby allowed.
2. THAT the Respondent is and hereby declared Vexatious
litigant.
3. THAT the Respondent herein David Oscar Owako is hereby
restrained from instituting in court any fresh legal proceedings
and or continuing any proceeding already instituted, without
leave of the Court.
4. THAT the Order herein shall on the authority of this Court be
published in the Kenya Gazette pursuant to section 2(3) of the
Vexatious Proceedings Act.
5. THAT each party shall bear the own costs of the publication.
6. THAT this file is accordingly closed.

GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15372
THE CONSTITUTION OF KENYA, 2010
ESTABLISHMENT OF SENATOR ANDREW OKIYA OMTATAH OKOITI
PRESIDENTIAL EXPLORATORY COMMITTEE
PURSUANT to Article 129, as read together with Article 138 of
the Constitution of Kenya, 2010, it is notified for the information of
the general public that Andrew Okiya Omtatah Okoiti, the Senator of
the Republic of Kenya elected by the great people of Busia County,
has established a Presidential Exploratory Committee (the
“Committee”).
The Committee’s primary objective is to gather, analyze, evaluate,
and act on information on the viability of the Senator’s presidential bid
for the August, 2027 General Election.
The Committee shall consist of:
Name Title Role
Mary Kathomi
Riungu (Ms.)
Chairperson To lead the coordination of
the Committee’s activities
and ensure timely delivery
of outputs.
Charles ole Kabaiku Deputy
Chairperson/
Diaspora
Coordinator
To assist the chairperson in
the execution of her
mandate and co-ordinate
Diaspora affairs.
David John Bwakali Political and
Grassroot
Strategist
To provide insights into
political trends, electoral
dynamics, and strategic
opportunities.
Victor Kipng’etich Legal Advisor To ensure compliance with
electoral laws, and advise on
legal matters.
Hanifa Adan Public
Relations/Media
Consultant
To manage relations with
the public and coordinate
with the media
Jude Ogulla Analytics lead To conduct opinion polls,
surveys, and data analysis
on political viability and
public sentiment.
THE KENYA GAZETTE 22nd November, 2024
5466
Purity Ndambuki
(Ms.)
Joint Secretaries To support and ensure the
smooth functioning of the
Committee.
Emmanuel Baraka
Kevin Migwe
Kimwatu
Spokesperson To communicate on behalf
of the Committee.
Julius Okara (Eng.) Patron To guide the Committee.
Terms of Reference (ToR)
(a) Political Viability Assessment:
(i) To conduct public opinion polls and surveys to assess the
aspirant’s popularity, name recognition, and electability;
(ii) Evaluate the current political landscape, including the
strengths and weaknesses of potential opponents; and,
(iii) Analyse national, regional, and demographical political
trends.
(iv) Assess public sentiment on key issues.
(b) Stakeholder Engagement:
(i) Engage with key political stakeholders, including political
party leaders, grassroots organizations, corporate bodies, civil
society and influential groups;
(ii) Organise meetings with potential allies, endorsers, and
influencers to gauge support for the aspirant’s bid; and,
(iii) Facilitate discussions with political analysts, think tanks, and
advisors for strategic input.
(c) Strategic Planning:
(i) Develop an initial campaign strategy;
(ii) Draft a high-level campaign roadmap outlining potential
alliances, timelines, and resources required; and,
(iii) Identify and recruit potential members of the campaign team,
including advisors, strategists, and key operational staff.
(d) Legal Compliance Oversight:
(i) Ensure adherence to the electoral laws and regulations of
Kenya, including the Political Parties Act and the Elections
Act;
(ii) Monitor compliance with campaign finance laws and ensure
timely reporting, where necessary, to the Independent
Electoral and Boundaries Commission (IEBC); and,
(iii) Provide legal advice to protect the aspirant from electoral
legal risks.
(e) Media and Public Relations:
(i) Conduct a media presence analysis, reviewing the aspirant’s
visibility and reputation across digital and traditional media;
(ii) Test messaging by developing and disseminating preliminary
public statements, speeches, and social media campaigns;
and,
(iii) Liaise with the media and manage public inquiries, focusing
on building true narratives around and about the aspirant.
(f) Fundraising and Resource Mobilisation:
(i) Assess the aspirant’s ability to raise campaign funds;
(ii) Develop and implement an initial fundraising strategy,
including outreach to potential donors; and,
(iii) Track and report all funds raised, ensuring compliance with
Kenyan electoral finance regulations
In the performance of its functions, the Committee shall: –
(a) Be properly constituted to transact its business with six(6)
members present to form the quorum;
(b) Hold regular meetings at such places and such times as the
Committee shall consider necessary for the proper discharge of
its mandate;
(c) Request for documentation and any other material or
information required in the discharge of its mandate;
(d) As it considers appropriate, carry out or cause to be carried out
such studies or research as may inform the Committee;
(e) Submit reports to the aspirant upon request; and,
(f) Subject to the foregoing, the committee shall have the
operational independence necessary for carrying out its
function, and for the proper execution of its mandate, including
the power to regulate its own procedures without being the
subject of control by any other person, body and/or authority.
(g) Have the power to co-opt professional expertise for specialised
aspects under its terms of reference;
(h) Submit to the aspirant a report with appropriate
recommendations at the end of its term.
Duration and Termination of the Committee
The Committee shall remain in office for a period not exceeding
eighteen (18) months from the date of the publication.
The Committee shall be dissolved immediately the aspirant
formally announces his candidacy or decision not to run. All activities,
assets, and records will be transferred to the appropriate campaign or
political entities as directed by the aspirant.
The appointments take effect from the date of this notice.
Dated the 22nd November, 2024.

DECREE
CLAIM FOR IN THE ORIGINATING SUMMON
1. Whether the 1st Respondent is the beneficial owner of the
2nd Respondent.
2. Whether the 2nd Respondent’s corporate veil should be
lifted/unmasked for engaging in Corruption and/or Money Laundering.
THE KENYA GAZETTE 22nd November, 2024
5500
3. Whether Kshs. 73,947,807.60 transacted through the 1st
Respondent's Bank Account Numbers 0160291143992 and
1110299529471 both held at Equity Bank Limited during the period of
interest constitute unexplained assets within the meaning of the
provisions of Section 55 of the ACECA.
4. Whether the Kshs. 82,083,066.00 transacted through the 2nd
Respondent’s Bank Account Number 111026450403 held at Equity
Bank Limited during the period of interest constitute unexplained
assets within the meaning of the provisions of Section 55 of the
ACECA.
5. In the alternative, whether the said Kshs. 73,947,807.60 and
Kshs. 82,083,066.00 stated in Paragraphs 3 and 4 above, respectively,
are proceeds of Corruption and or proceeds of money laundering.
6. Whether the Respondents jointly and severally should be
ordered to pay to the Government of Kenya the cumulative sum of
Kshs. 156,030,873.60 being cash deposits and bank transfers
enumerated in paragraph 3 and 4 herein above and interest thereon at
Court rates from the date of filing this suit until payment in full.
7. Whether the suit immovable properties and developments
thereon constitute unexplained assets within the meaning of the
provisions of Section 55 of the ACECA:
(a) Dundori/Lanet Block 5/2232 (Kiamunyeki “A”)

(i) Limuru/Kirenga/T. 225

provisions of Section 55 of the Anti-Corruption and Economic Crimes

(a) KCB 253B - Tata Tipper
(b) KCH 101P - Toyota Station/ Wagon
(c) KCF 410P - NISSAN Station/Wagon

of the movable and immovable assets described in paragraphs VII and

of this suit.
THIS MATTER coming up for Judgment on 26th September, 2024
before Lady Justice E.N. Maina for the Originating Summons dated
18th February, 2020 brought by Counsel for the Applicant under
Section 55 of the Anti-Corruption and Economic Crimes Act
(ACECA) and Section 11 (I) (j) of the Ethics and Anti-Corruption
Commission Act and all enabling provisions of the law, IN THE
PRESENCE of Counsel for the Applicant and Interested parties,
IT IS HEREBY DECREED;
1. THAT the Respondents are jointly and severally hereby
ordered to pay to the Government of Kenya the sum of Kshs.
82,083,066/- which was deposited into the 2nd Respondent's account
during the period of Interest.
2. THAT the following immovable properties and the
developments thereon constitute unexplained assets within the
meaning of Section 55 of the ACECA and hence be and are hereby
forfeited to the Government.
(i) Dundori/Lanet Block 5/2232 (Kiamunyeki 'A')
(ii) Dundori/Lanet Block 5/1014 (Kiamunyeki 'A')
(iii) Dagorreti/Kinoo/3213
(iv) Dagoretti/Kinoo/4469
(v) Kiambu/Municipality/Block 1/233
(vi) Limuru/Kirenga/T.218
(vii) Limuru/Kirenga/T.225
3. THAT in regard to properties Dagorretti/Kino0/47 and
Kikuyu/Kikuyu Block 1/891 the same be and are hereby forfeited less
the value of the current market value of the land upon which the
developments stand.
4. THAT the below listed motor vehicles be and are hereby
forfeited to the State: -
(i) KCB 253B - Tata Tipper
(ii) KCH 101P - Toyota Station/ Wagon
(iii) KCF 410P - Nissan Station/Wagon
(iv) KCE 039L - Isuzu Truck
5. THAT in the alternative to order (b), (c) and (d) above the
Respondents shall pay to the Government of Kenya an amount equal
to the market value of the moveable and immovable properties as
stated in the Commission's valuation report.
6. THAT the Respondents shall pay the Commission's and 11th
Interested Party's costs.

BEFORE HON. LADY JUSTICE B. M. MUSYOKI
ORDER
CLAIMS IN THE APPLICATION
1. This Application be and is hereby certified urgent and the same
be heard ex parte in the first instance.
2. This Honourable Court be and is hereby pleased to issue an
order in the nature of a preservation order restraining, enjoining or
prohibiting the Respondents whether by themselves or their agents,
servants, employees or otherwise howsoever from selling, transferring,
disposing off, leasing, charging, encumbering, sequestrating, taking
possession of, altering or interfering with the ownership of or
otherwise dealing with all that parcel of land known as L.R. No.
Nairobi Block 157/826 (formerly L.R. No. Kiambu/ Dandora/651
(New Survey No. 826).
3. This Honourable Court be and is hereby pleased to issue an
order directing the Chief Land Registrar to enter or place a restriction
against the register of all that parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi
Block 157/826 (formerly L.R. No. Kiambu/ Dandora/651 (New
Survey No. 826)) restricting or prohibiting any dealings in the said
parcel of land including transferring, charging, encumbering, leasing
or sequestration without the consent of this Honourable Court.
4. This Honourable Court be and is hereby pleased to issue an
order in the nature of a preservation order restraining, enjoining or
prohibiting the Respondents whether by themselves or their agents,
servants, employees or otherwise howsoever from receiving or
collecting such rents payable on account of the residential building
erected on all that parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi Block
157/826 (formerly L.R. No. Kiambu/Dandora/651 (New Survey No.
826)) without the consent of this Honourable Court.
5. This Honourable Court be and is hereby pleased to issue an
order directing the Respondents to hand over or surrender the
certificate of ownership or title documents issued in respect of all that
parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi Block 157/826 (formerly
L.R. No. Kiambu/ Dandora/651 (New Survey No. 826)) to the Agency
THE KENYA GAZETTE 22nd November, 2024
5502
Director or such officer as the Agency Director may nominate for safe
keeping and management within 7 days of service of this order.
6. This Honourable Court be and is hereby pleased to issue an
order directing such tenants or occupiers of the residential building
erected on all that parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi Block
157/826 (formerly L.R. No. Kiambu/ Dandora/651 (New Survey No.
826)) to pay or remit such rent payable into the Criminal Assets
Recovery Fund bank account details:
Account Name: Criminal Assets Recovery Fund
Account Number: 1240221339
Bank Name: Kenya Commercial Bank
7. Costs of this suit; and
8. Any other relief or order that this Honourable Court may deem
just and appropriate to issue.
Ex-Parte;
UPON READING the Originating Motion dated 7th November,
2024 brought under certificate of urgency by Counsel for the
Applicant under Sections 53(1), 81(1) and (2), 82 (1) and (2) and 87
(1) and (2) and (3) of the Proceeds of Crime and Anti-Money
Laundering Act and Order 51 rule 1 of the Civil Procedure Rules,
2010, Article 40 (6) of the Constitution, 2010 and all enabling
provisions of the law, AND UPON READING the supporting affidavit
of Martin Samburumo sworn on 7th November, 2024 and the
annextures thereto;
IT IS HEREBY ORDERED
1. THAT a Preservation Order be and is hereby issued restraining,
enjoining or prohibiting the Respondents whether by themselves or
their agents, servants, employees or otherwise howsoever from selling,
transferring, disposing off, leasing, charging, encumbering,
sequestrating, taking possession of, altering or interfering with the
ownership of or otherwise dealing with all that parcel of land known as
L.R. No. Nairobi Block 157/826 (formerly L.R. No.
Kiambu/Dandora/651 (New Survey No. 826).
2. THAT an Order be and is hereby issued directing the Chief
Land Registrar to enter or place a restriction against the register of all
that parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi Block 157/826
(formerly L.R. No. Kiambu/Dandora/651 (New Survey No. 826))
restricting or prohibiting any dealings in the said parcel of land
including transferring, charging, encumbering, leasing or sequestration
without the consent of this Honourable Court.
3. THAT a preservation order be and is hereby issued restraining,
enjoining or prohibiting the Respondents whether by themselves or
their agents, servants, employees or otherwise howsoever from
receiving or collecting such rents payable on account of the residential
building erected on all that parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi
Block 157/826 (formerly L.R. No. Kiambu/Dandora/651 (New Survey
No. 826) without the consent of this Honourable Court.
4. This Honourable Court be and is hereby pleased to issue an
order directing the Respondents to hand over or surrender the
certificate of ownership or title documents issued in respect of all that
parcel of land known as L.R. No. Nairobi Block 157/826 (formerly
L.R. No. Kiambu/ Dandora/651 (New Survey No. 826)) to the Agency
Director or such officer as the Agency Director may nominate for safe
keeping and management within 7 days of service of this order.
5. THAT an order be and is hereby issued directing such tenants
or occupiers of the residential building erected on all that parcel of
land known as L.R. No. Nairobi Block 157/826 (formerly L.R. No.
Kiambu/ Dandora/651 (New Survey No. 826) to pay or remit such rent
payable into the Criminal Assets Recovery Fund bank account details:
Account Name: Criminal Assets Recovery Fund
Account Number: 1240221339
Bank Name: Kenya Commercial Bank
6. THAT costs of the application shall be in the cause.
7. THAT orders granted shall be effective for (90) ninety days
from the date of publication of this order in the Kenya Gazette as by
law required.
GIVEN under my hand and the seal of this Honorable Court this
12th day of November, 2024.
ISSUED at NAIROBI this 13th day of November, 2024.

NATIONAL ENVIRONMENT MANAGEMENT AUTHORITY
ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT ASSESSMENT STUDY REPORT
FOR THE PROPOSED RESIDENTIAL APARTMENTS COMPLEX
ON PLOT L.R. NO. NAIROBI/BLOCK 5/646 ALONG GROVE
LANE, WESTLANDS, NAIROBI CITY COUNTY
INVITATION OF PUBLIC COMMENTS
PURSUANT to section 59 of the Environmental Management and
Coordination Act, 1999, the National Environment Management
Authority (NEMA) has received an Environmental Impact Assessment
Study Report for the above proposed project.
The proponent, Clermont Development Limited, proposes to
develop a residential block of three basements for parking space,
ground floor to 22nd floors comprising of 484No. Residential
apartment Units (327 No. one bedroom, 42 No. two bedroom, 91 No.
three bedroom, 24 No. studios), perimeter wall, gate, other associated
amenities and facilities on Apartments Complex on Plot L.R. No.
Nairobi/Block 5/646 along Grove Lane, Westlands, Nairobi City
County.
The following are the anticipated impacts and proposed mitigation
measures–
Impacts Proposed Mitigation Measure
Air pollution
• All personnel, working at the project site will be
trained prior to starting construction on methods
for minimizing air quality impact during
construction.
• All workers at the site will be required to wear
protective clothing while on duty.
• Traffic speed of construction machineries will be
restricted to maximum of 15 mph
• Exposed stockpiles of e.g. dust and sand, will be
enclosed covered, and watered daily, or treated
with non-toxic soil blinders
• All active construction areas will be watered at
least twice per week reduce dust
• All construction areas will be covered by
scaffolding/dust nets to provide optimal dust
protection
• Construction vehicles drivers will be under strict
instruction to minimize unnecessary trips and
minimize idling of the engines.
• Ensure that the manuals of operations are
followed to the latter to avoid possible accidents
• Vegetation will be replanted in disturbed areas as
soon as possible to create green space and
stabilizes soils
Noise
pollution
• Construction works to be carried out during
daytime only
• Use of very noisy equipment will be limited to
daytime only
• Regular maintenance of machinery to minimize
noise.
• Use of equipment designed with noise control
elements will be adopted where necessary.
• All workers operating in noisy areas or operating
noisy equipment will be provided with earmuffs
to protect against extreme noise.
• Idling time for pick-up trucks and other small
equipment will be minimized to limited time.
• Encourage use of manual labor where appropriate.
Generation of
solid waste
• Wastes to be collected regularly to control air
pollution and vermin/insects etc.
• All persons involved in refuse collection shall be
in full protective attire.
22nd November, 2024 THE KENYA GAZETTE 5507
Impacts Proposed Mitigation Measure
• Ensure that all solid wastes are collected and
segregated
• Contact a licensed solid waste transported to
collect solid materials leakages and/or spillages
• Minimize waste at source by clear and
harmonized procurement materials
• Resource recovery will be encouraged once the
project takes off so as to shrink waste stream and
recover non recyclables
• For hazardous waste- In case of spillage
emergency spillage control measures to be
instituted.
Health and
safety
concerns.
• Conduct regular training on work safety
procedures
• Provide appropriate personnel Protective
Equipment (PPE) to site workers
• Provide for appropriate signage and warnings at
work sites
• Provide for First Aid facilities as per the OSHA,
2007.
• Establish secure site access points to control entry
and prevent unauthorized access.
• Conduct training sessions on health and safety
practices, emergency response, and specific
hazards associated with the construction project.
• Maintain records of any injuries or incidents and
follow up with appropriate corrective measures.
Water and
energy
resources.
• Install rainwater harvesting systems for landscape
irrigation and non-potable uses to reduce demand
on NAWASCO water.
• Reuse recycled water as much as possible with
proper treatment procedures
• Regularly test nearby water bodies for turbidity,
pH, and pollutants to identify and address
contamination from construction activities.
• Install solar photovoltaic (PV) panels on rooftops
or common areas to generate renewable
electricity for common facilities
• Record water consumption during construction to
identify waste and optimize usage.
Climate
change
• Include climate change prevention measures in
environmental policy and sensitize workers on
importance of adherence to the measures
• Use low-carbon materials, such as recycled steel,
sustainably sourced timber, and low-emission
concrete alternatives for carbon footprint
reduction.
• Use electric or hybrid construction vehicles and
equipment where feasible.
• Elevate buildings or critical equipment areas
above known flood levels, and design drainage
systems to handle heavy rainfall.
• Use cool paints and roofing materials with high
solar reflectance to reduce urban heat buildup
around the site.
Impacts Proposed Mitigation Measure
Water
pollution
• Storm water management plan that minimizes
impervious area increases infiltration by use of
recharge areas, and use of retention, and/or
retention with graduated outlet control structures,
will be used.
• Keep natural water channels free from
obstruction.
• Maintain internal and immediate external
drainage systems clear all the times
• Undertake roof catchment harvesting to reduce
volumes of storm water
Traffic
disruption
• Provide a traffic flow management plan to avoid
traffic congestions and accidents
• Clearly display speed limit signs in critical areas,
such as pedestrian crossings, entry points, and
around blind corners.
• Address complaints promptly and adjust traffic
management strategies if necessary. Document
any traffic incidents or complaints and implement
corrective actions where necessary.
Accidents • Designate specific work zones for tasks
performed at height and mark them clearly.
• Limit access to these zones to only authorized
and trained personnel.
• Use safety nets and catch platforms under high-
risk areas to prevent injuries from falling object.
• Provide specialized training to all workers on
height safety, proper use of PPE, and fall arrest
techniques.
• Erect perimeter fencing and signage around the
construction site to prevent public access to high-
risk areas. Install warning signs at site entrances
and around high-risk zones to alert the public and
workers to height-related hazards.

INVITATION OF PUBLIC COMMENTS
PURSUANT to section 59 of the Environmental Management and
Co-ordination (Impact Assessment and Audit) Regulations, 2003, the
National Environment Management Authority (NEMA) has received
an Environmental Impact Assessment Study Report for the above-
proposed project.
The proponent, Freight Lane Hotel Limited, proposes to construct
a five storey (ground floor, mezzanine plus five floors hotel block
comprising of 180No. guest accommodation rooms of varying
typologies), all day dining facilities, lobby bar, meeting rooms, gym,
rooftop swimming pool ,108No.parking spaces, other associated
facilities and amenities on Plot No. L.R. 9042/315 within Jomo
Kenyatta International Airport, off Mombasa Road, Embakasi Sub-
County, Nairobi City County.
The following are the anticipated impacts and proposed mitigation
measures:
Environmental Impacts Mitigation Measures
Increased noise and
vibrations generation
• Employ sound insulation technologies
work effectively towards minimizing
high noise levels from the external
environment in the vicinity.
• Ensure that all vehicles and construction
machinery are well maintained and
regularly serviced to avoid excessive
noise generation.
• Provide appropriate protective gear for
all construction workers working in
noisy sections.
• The delivery of construction materials
and noisy activities should be done,
preferably at off-peak hours to minimize
high level noise impacts.
• Ensure compliance with the
Environmental Management and
Coordination (Noise and Excessive
Vibration Pollution) (Control)
Regulations, 2009.
Increased solid waste
generation
• Use of an Integrated Solid Waste
Management System (ISWMS); through
a hierarchy of options including source
reduction, recycling, composting and
reuse.
• Efficient estimation and use of building
material to reduce waste and
recycling/reuse where feasible.
Environmental Impacts Mitigation Measures
• Engage the services of a NEMA licensed
waste handler to collect and transport
waste to designated disposal sites.
• Manage all waste in line with the
requirements of the Environmental
Management and Co-ordination (Waste
Management) Regulations, 2006 and the
Sustainable Waste Management Act,
2022.
Increased generation of
waste water
• Installation of adequate mobile toilets
separate for males and females that are
well-maintained and have adequate hand
washing facilities.
• Water containing pollutants such as
cement, concrete, lime, chemicals, and
fuels to be discharged into a conservancy
tank for removal from the site.
• Comply with the provisions of the
Environmental Management and
Coordination (Water Quality)
Regulations, 2006.
Air pollution • Regular sprinkling of water on work
areas to prevent fugitive dust violations.
• Use of dust nets/screens around the
construction site to contain and arrest
dust.
• All construction machinery should be
regularly serviced to minimize the
generation of hazardous gases.
• Sensitize construction drivers and
machinery operators to switch off
engines when not in use.
• Regularly monitor air quality levels to
ensure compliance with Environmental
Management and Coordination (Air
Quality) Regulations, 2014.
Traffic congestion and
accidents
• Adopt a Traffic Management plan and
Delivery Management Plan to enhance
traffic movement within the site and
along the third freight lane.
• Minimize haulage and transportation of
construction material during peak hours.
• Construct acceleration and deceleration
lanes to channel delivery trucks to the
site without creating a backlog of traffic
behind them as they navigate turns of
entry.
• Proper signage and warnings should be
placed at strategic locations.
• Construction activities should be done
within the confines of the construction
area.
• Any change in the normal programming
of activities that will significantly disrupt
normalcy along the abutting project
roads should be timely communicated.
Occupational health
and safety risks
• Registration of the site prior to
commencement of construction works.
• Provide appropriate PPE to workers.
• Training of workers in Occupational
Safety and Health and Construction
Safety.
• Obtain a Work Injury Benefit Act
(WIBA) cover for employees.
THE KENYA GAZETTE 22nd November, 2024
5510

• Ensure compliance with the
Occupational Safety and Health Act
(OSHA), 2007.
• Hire a qualified safety officer to oversee

Aviation safety • Avoid the use of reflective materials due

GAZETTE NOTICE NO. 15542
THE INSOLVENCY ACT, 2015
(Subsection 48 (3) of the Insolvency Act)
IN THE HIGH COURT OF KENYA AT NAIROBI
IN INSOLVENCY CAUSE NO. E17 OF 2023
IN THE MATTER OF MUNGAI KARANJA–Debtor
BANKRUPTCY ORDER
MADE ON 17TH DAY OF OCTOBER, 2024
BANKRUPTCY
TAKE NOTICE THAT:
A Bankruptcy Order was made against Mungai Karanja on the
17th day of October, 2024 and the Official Receiver, was appointed as
trustee of the estate of the bankrupt by the court; subject to affirmation
by the creditors of the trustee’s appointment or substitution of another
trustee by the creditors.
Pursuant to section 254 (1) of the Insolvency Act, the bankrupt will
be given an automatic discharge on the 17th day of October, 2027
unless the Official Receiver, the trustee of the estate of the bankrupt or
a creditor of the bankrupt gives notice of intended opposition to the
discharge of the bankrupt before that date.
Any creditor who intends to oppose the discharge of the bankrupt
shall state in writing the grounds for his/her opposition and send a
notice to this effect to the Official Receiver, the trustee of the estate of
the bankrupt and the bankrupt at any time before the 17th day of
October, 2027.
If the discharge of the bankrupt is opposed, the trustee will apply
to the Court without delay for an appointment for the hearing of the
opposition in the manner prescribed by the Act and the Insolvency
Regulations.
Dated the 24th October, 2024.
MARK GAKURU,
MR/6535499 Official Receiver/Bankruptcy Trustee.

2024 to send full particulars of all the claims they may have against
the company to the undersigned, the joint liquidators of the company,
and if so required, by notice in writing from the joint liquidators,

claims set out in such notice, or in default thereof, they may be
excluded from the benefit of any distribution made before such debts
are proved.
The joint liquidators act as agents of the company without any
personal liability.
Muniu Thoithi and George Weru,

GOVERNMENT PRESS ON E-CITIZEN PLATFORM
ACCESSING SERVICES ON E-CITIZEN PLATFORM
The following steps can be used by Users who have an existing
eCitizen account. If you do not have an eCitizen account please create
one before attempting to follow these steps.
STEPS
1. Open the following URL on your browser
https://governmentpress.ecitizen.go.ke/ and press enter.
2. Click on sign in button.
3. Click login with e-Citizen.
4. Enter your National ID Number and your password and click Sign
In.
5. Choose/click where you want to receive the one time password
OTP code. That is your phone or Email.
6. Enter the OTP sent to your Email or Phone and click next.
7. Select the account to login with and click continue.
8. Select the services that you want.
TO PURCHASE A PUBLICATION
1. Click on Sale of Publication and then click next in the resulting
page.
2. Enter your address details and click next.
3. Click the drop down arrow to choose the type of publication you
wish to purchase and click preview.
4. Confirm your details and the details of the publication and click
complete.
TO PLACE KENYA GAZETTE ADVERTISEMENT
1. Click Kenya Gazette advertisement and then click next in the
resulting page.
2. Click next on the applicants details page.
3. Click the drop down arrow to select the size of the advertisement
page/space, Select the date for advertisement and click next.
Note: It take two (2) weeks for the advertisement to be placed.
4. Click next.
5. Confirm advert size and click next.
6. Click choose file to upload the advertisement text and click
preview.
7. Review your application and click complete.
8. Await for 222 222 Prompt for payment on your PHONE.
9. Proceed and make Payment.
10. Print the receipt.
Note: For gazette advertisement, hard copy MUST be availed to the
Cash Office attached with a copy of the GOVERNMENT
COPY receipt download from the e-citizen account.
CONTACT US:
E-mail: gazette@governmentpress.go.ke
Tel. 020 2211278
IMPORTANT NOTICE TO SUBSCRIBERS TO
THE KENYA GAZETTE
THE following notes are for the guidance of persons submitting “copy” for
inclusion in the Kenya Gazette, Supplement, etc.:
(1) The Kenya Gazette contains Notices of a general nature which do not
affect legislation. They are, therefore, submitted to the Government
Printer directly.
(2) Legislative Supplement contains Rules and Regulations which are
issued by the National or County Governments. Because of this, they
must be submitted to the Government Printer through the office of the
Attorney–General.
(3) Bill Supplement contains Bills which are for introduction in the
National Assembly, Senate or County Assemblies.
(4) Act Supplement contains Acts passed by the National Assembly,
Senate or County Assemblies.
All “copy” submitted for publication should be prepared on one side of an
A4 sheet no matter how small the Notice is, each page being numbered and
should be typed with double spacing. Copy should be Clear, legible and contain
no alterations.
Particular attention should be paid to the following points:
(i) Signature must be supported by rubber–stamping or typing the name
of the signatory in capital letters.
(ii) Must be correct and filled in where necessary.
(iii) Care should be taken to ensure that all headings to Notices and
references to legislation are up to date and conform with the Revised
Edition of the Laws of Kenya.
EXTRACT FROM THE HUMAN RESOURCE POLICIES AND PROCEDURES MANUAL FOR
THE PUBLIC SERVICE —
Kenya Gazette
A.30 (1) All communication for publication in the Kenya Gazette should
reach the Government Printer not later than Friday of the week before
publication is desired.
(2) A State Department will be required to meet the cost of advertising in the
Kenya Gazette.
It is emphasized that these notes are for guidance only, but it is requested
that persons submitting copy for publication first satisfy themselves that such
copy is complete in every respect.
